तीन वर्ष की अवधि के लिए अथवा अगले आदेशों तक, जो भी पहले हो, केनरा बैंक में कार्यपालक निदेशक के पद पर 
नियुक्त करती है।

का.आ. 2220.--केंद्रीय सरकार एतद्‌ द्वारा दिल्‍ली विशेष पुलिस स्थापन अधिनियम, 7946 ((946 का केन्द्रीय 
अधिनियम 25) की धारा 5 की उप-धारा (4) सपठित धारा 6 द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए आदेश संख्या 
एचडी 94 सीओडी 2023, बेंगलुरु, दिनांक 42.02.2024 एवं अनुशेष आदेश संख्या:एचडी 94 सीओडी 2023, बेंगलुरु, 
दिनांक 47.40.2025 के माध्यम से जारी कर्नाटक राज्य सरकार की सम्मति से पॉपुलर फ़ाइनेंस ग्रुप ऑफ कंपनी के 
निदेशकों, साझेदारों और मालिकों तथा बेंगलुरु के विभिन्न पुलिस स्टेशनों में मेसर्स पॉपुलर फ़ाइनेंस ग्रुप ऑफ कंपनीज के 


विरुद्ध माननीय प्रधान नगर सिविल एवं सत्र न्यायाधीश, बेंगलुरु सिटी के दिनांक 03.09.2025 के आदेश द्वारा अद्यतन 
किए गए विधि एवं अधिनियमों की संगत धाराओं सहित दर्ज 66 एफआईआर के संदर्भ में कथित धोखाधड़ी में संलिप्त 
व्यक्तियों द्वारा किए गए करोड़ों रुपये की धोखाधड़ी का अन्वेषण करने के लिए दिल्‍ली विशेष पुलिस स्थापन के सदस्यों की 
शक्तियों और क्षेत्राधिकार का विस्तार करती है। दिनांक 03.09.2025 के आदेश द्वारा अद्यतन किए गए विधि एवं 
अधिनियमों की संगत धाराओं सहित दर्ज 66 एफआईआर की सूची जो अनुबंध-क के रूप में संलग्न है। उक्त 66 एफआईआर 


में 65 एफआईआर को माननीय प्रधान नगर सिविल एवं सत्र न्यायाधीश, बेंगलुरु सिटी के दिनांक 09.4.2023 के 
आदेश द्वारा बेंगलुरु के यशवंतपुर पुलिस स्टेशन में दर्ज अपराध संख्या 768/2020 के साथ जोड़ दिया गया था |

7654 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JANUARY 3, 2026/PAUSHA 3, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


New Delhi, the 9th December, 2025 


S.O. 2220.—In exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section () of section 5 read with section 6 of the 
Delhi Special Police Establishment Act, 946 (Central Act 25 of !946), the Central Government with the consent of 
the State Government of Karnataka, issued vide order No. HD 94 COD 2023, Bengaluru dated 2.02.2024 and 
Addendum Order No:HD 94 COD 2023, Bengaluru dated 7.!0.2025, hereby extends the powers and jurisdiction of 
the members of the Delhi Special Police Establishment for investigation of multi-crores fraud committed by Directors, 
Partners and Owners of Popular Finance Group of Companies and the persons involved in connection with alleged 
fraud in respect of 66 FIRs registered against M/s. Popular Finance Group Companies at various police stations at 
Bengaluru alongwith corresponding sections of law and Acts as updated vide order dated 03.09.2025 of the Hon’ble 
Principal City Civil and Session Judge, Bengaluru City. The list of 66 FIRs along with corresponding sections of law 
and acts as updated vide order dated 03.09.2025 is attached as Annexure-A. Out of 66 FIRs, 65 of the said FIRs were 
clubbed with Crime No. !68/2020 registered at Yeshwanthapura Police Station Bengaluru vide order dated 09..2023 
of the Hon’ble Principal City Civil and Session Judge, Bengaluru City. 


[F. No. 228/95/2025-AVD-I]]

का.आ. 2224.--केन्द्रीय सरकार एतद्द्वारा दिल्‍ली विशेष पुलिस स्थापन अधिनियम, 946 (946 का केन्द्रीय 
अधिनियम 25) की धारा 5 की उप-धारा () सपठित धारा 6 द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए आदेश सं. एचडी 
404 सीओडी 2025, बेंगलुरू, दिनांक 07.4.2025 के माध्यम से जारी कर्नाटक राज्य की सम्मति से गूगल प्ले स्टोर में 
“गलद अरब” एप्लीकेशन के मालिकों, निर्माणकर्ताओं एवं प्रचालकों तथा अन्य अज्ञात व्यक्तियों के विरूद्ध भारतीय न्याय 


संहिता, 2023 की धारा 6(2), 744 एवं 204 और सूचना प्रौद्योगिकी अधिनियम, 2000 की धारा 66डी के तहत 


अपराध(धों) तथा अन्वेषण के दौरान सामने आने वाले अन्य अपराध का पंजीकरण एवं अन्वेषण करने के लिए दिल्‍ली 
विशेष पुलिस स्थापन के सदस्यों की शक्तियों और क्षेत्राधिकार का विस्तार करती है।

JUDGMENT 


By means of order/reference no. L-402/27/20I2-IR(BI) dated 30.08.20l2, the Central Government 
considered it desirable to refer the dispute for adjudication to the following effect:- 


"Whether the action of the management of Northern Railway, Muradabad in terminating the services 
of Shri Sheetala Prasad S/o Shri Ramanand w. e. f 22/2/986, is legal and justified? To what relief the 
workman is entitled?" 


In pursuance to the said reference I.D. Case No. 68/202 registered before this tribunal. 


Thereafter claimant filed his statement of claim before this tribunal on 24.09.202 in which it has been stated that 
Shri Sheetal Prasad/ workman was initially engaged as casual labour on l6.0i.975 and in the said capacity work till 
2.2.986 however, his services were retrenched/termination on 22.2.986. 


Further in claim statement the relief as claimed by workman is that his termination/retrenchment, order dated 
22.2.[986 may be set aside, he may be reinstated service with all constitutional benefit. 


The respondent has filed written statement and thereafter the rejoinder and documents/evidence have been exchanged 
between parties. 


[भाग [I—Ve 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जनवरी 3, 2026/07 3, 947 7667 


On behalf of respondent a plea was taken that as per the case of workman, his services was retrenchment on 
22.2.986, for redressed of his grievances approached the authority in the year 202. 


Thereafter reference was made on 30.08.202, so the case filed by the workman is able to be dismissed on the ground 
of delay and he is not entitled for any relief. 


FINDING AND CONCLUSION 


As per to the admitted facts of the present case, the claimant/workman was engaged as casual labour on 24.09.202, 
and his services was retrenched/terminated on 22.2.986. 


Thereafter for redressal of his grievances approached the authority in the year 202, the present reference has been 
made on 30.08.202, filed his claim statement on 24.09.202, before this tribunal. 


In view of the said facts the claim statement filed by workman against his retrenchment/termination, dated 
22.2.986, was after l6 years, so the same is highly belated as such he is not entitled for any relief in view of the law 
which are quoted here in below: 


In Nedungadi Bank Ltd. vs. K.P. Madhavankutty and Ors. 2000 (2) SCC 455 it was noted at paragraph 6 as 
follows: 


"6. Law does not prescribe any time-limit for the appropriate Government to exercise its powers under 
Section 709 of the Act. It is not that this power can be exercised at any point of time and to revive matters 
which had since heel) settled. Power is to be exercised reasonably and in a rational manner. There appears 
to us to be no rational basis on which the Central Government has exercised powers in this case after a 
lapse of about seven years of the order dismissing the respondent from ser-vice. At the time reference was 
made no industrial dispute existed or could be even said to have been apprehended. A dispute which is stale 
could not be the subject-matter of reference under Section 79 of the Act. As to when a dispute can be said to 
be stale would depend on the facts and circumstances of each case. When the matter has become final, it 
appears to us to be rather incongruous that the reference be made under Section 79 of the Act in the 
circumstances like the present one. In fact it could be said that there was no dispute pending at the time 
when the reference in question was made. The only ground advanced by the respondent was that two other 
employees who were dismissed from service were reinstated. Under what circumstances they were dismissed 
and subsequently reinstated is nowhere mentioned. Demand raised by the respondent for raising an 
industrial dispute was ex-facie bad and incompetent." 


Further, in S.M. Nilajkar and Ors. v. Telecom District Manager, Karnataka 2003 (4) SCC 27 the position was 
reiterated as follows: (at para 7) 


#77, It was submitted on behalf of the respondent that on account of delay in raising the dispute by the 
appellants the High Court was justified in denying relief to the appellants. We cannot agree. It is true, as 
held in M/s. Shalimar Works Ltd. v. Their Workmen (supra) AIR 7959 SC 7277), that merely because the 
Industrial Disputes Act does not pro-vide for a limitation for raising the dispute it does not mean that the 
dispute can be raised at any time and without regard to the delay and reasons therefor. There is no 
limitation prescribed for reference of disputes to an industrial tribunal, even so it is only reasonable that the 
disputes should be referred as soon as possible after they have arisen and after conciliation proceedings 
have failed particularly so when disputes relate to discharge of workmen wholesale. A delay of 4 years in 
raising the dispute after even reemployment of the most of the old workmen was held to be fatal in M/ s. 
Shalimar Works Limited v. Their Workmen (supra) AIR 7959 SC 7277, In Nedungadi Bank Ltd. v. K.P. 
Madhavankutty and others (supra) AIR 2000 SC 839, a delay of 7 years was held to be fatal and disentitled 
to workmen to any relief. In Ratan Chandra Sammanta and others v Union of India and others (supra) /993 
AIR SCW 2244, it was held that a casual labourer retrenched by the employer deprives himself of remedy 
available in law by delay itself, lapse of time results in losing the remedy and the right as well. The delay 
would certainly be fatal if it has resulted in material evidence relevant to adjudication being lost and 
rendered not available. However, we do not think that the delay in the case at hand has been so culpable as 
to disentitle the appellants for any relief. Although the High Court has opined that there was a delay of 7 to 9 
years in raising the dispute before the Tribunal but we find the High Court factually not correct. The 
employment of the appellants was terminated sometime in /965-86 or /986-67., Pursuant to the judgment in 
Daily Rated Casual Employees Under P&T Department v. Union of India (supra) AIR 7/967 SC 2342, the 
department was formulating a scheme to accommodate casual labourers and the appellants were justified in 
awaiting the outcome thereof. On 76-7-/999 they were refused to be accommodated in the scheme. On 28- 
42-/990 they initiated the proceedings under the Industrial Disputes Act followed by conciliation 
proceedings and then the dispute was referred to the Industrial Tribunal cum-Labour Court. We do not think 
that the appellants deserve to be non suited on the ground of delay." 


7668 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JANUARY 3, 2026/PAUSHA 3, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


The Hon'ble Apex Court in the case of Krishi Utpadan Mandi Samity vs. Pahal Singh reported in 2007 2 SCC 
93 and more particularly paragraph Nos. l0, l, which read as under:- 


709, The Labour Court was also under an obligation to consider as to whether any relief, if at all could be 
granted in favour of the workman in view of the fact that the industrial dispute had been raised after ॥6 
years. It was obligatory on the part of the Labour Court to consider that the respondent was in employment 
for very short period. It had also not arrived at a finding that the respondent was in continuous service 
within the meaning of Section 2(g) of the U.P. Industrial Disputes Act or for that matter in terminating the 
ser-vices of the respondent, the appellant did not comply with the requirements of law particularly Section 6- 
N thereof. In absence of such a finding, the High Court in our opinion should have interfered with the 
Award. 


३. It is now well-settled principle of law that "delay de-feats equity". 
(see also Haryana State Co-operative Land Development Bank vs. Neelam (2005) 5 SCC 97 ) 


Further, in the present case, it is also not in dispute rather admitted by the workman has raised his grievance to be 
included in screening for year 983-84 by means of raising industrial dispute in the year 2003, which was referred to 
this Tribunal after 20 years, as such, he is not entitled for any relief. Because, Hon'ble Rajasthan High Court, Jaipur 
Bench, in the case of M.D./Chief Manager, Jaipur Agar, Rajasthan State Road Transport Corp., Jaipur vs. 
General Secretary, Rajasthan Transport Workers Organisation, Jaipur, 2024 (82) FLR 892, has held as under: 


"6. In the case at hand, the respondent-workman was appointed on the post of Driver vide order dated 
47.72./966. Accordingly, the benefit of first selection scale became due after nine years, sometime in 2004 - 
2005. The same was not done and neither was the non-grant of the benefit at the time was challenged by the 
respondent-workman. The benefit of first selection scale was only granted vide order dated 06.04.2004. This 
deferment was also not challenged immediately and was only challenged for the first time in 20/3, The first 
issue that is to be decided by this Court is what effect, if any, would this delay have on the merits of the case. 


7. To decide the first issue, recourse may be taken to Hon'ble Supreme Court judgment of Mohan Lal (supra), the 
relevant portion of which is reproduced as under: 


7॥9, We are clearly of the view that though the Limitation Act, 963 is not applicable to the reference made 
under the ID Act but delay in raising industrial dispute is definitely an important circumstance which the 
Labour Court must keep in view at the time of exercise of discretion irrespective of whether or not such 
objection has been raised by the other side. The legal position laid down by this Court in Gitam Singh [Asstt. 
Eng., Rajasthan Development Corpn. v. Gitam Singh], that before exercising its judicial discretion, the 
Labour Court has to keep in view all relevant factors including the mode and manner of appointment, nature 
of employment, length of service, of service, the ground on which termination has been set aside and the 
delay in raising industrial dispute before grant of relief in an industrial dispute, must be invariably followed. 


20. Now, if the facts of the present case are seen, the position that emerges is this: the workman worked as a 
work-charged employee for a period from 7./7./964 to 77.2./966 (in all he worked for 286 days during his 
employment). The services of the workman were terminated with effect from 46., 2, /986, The workman raised 
the industrial dispute in /992 i.e. after 6 years of termination. The Labour Court did not keep in view 
admitted delay of 6 years in raising the industrial dispute by the workman. The judicial discretion exercised 
by the Labour Court is, thus, flawed and unsustainable. The Division Bench of the High Court was clearly in 
error in restoring the award of the Labour Court whereby reinstatement was granted to the workman. 
Though, the compensation awarded by the Single Judge was too low and needed to be enhanced by the 
Division Bench but surely reinstatement of the workman in the facts and circumstances is not the 
appropriate relief." 


Further, the Hon'ble Supreme Court in Sadhu Singh (supra), held as under: 


"6. We shall at the outset deal with the issue of limitation. The respondent was retired compulsorily from 
service on 4.47.2093. Original Civil Suit No. 47 of 2079 was instituted in 2070, The trial Judge as well as the 
first appellate court were of the view that the suit was not barred by limitation since the representation of the 
respondent for the grant of the three selection grades was rejected on 76.7/.2070, The first appellate court, 
while concurring with the trial Judge also noted that the "final request" made by the respondent-plaintiff on 
46.47.2040 was rejected and hence the suit was within limitation. 


7. The respondent waited for seven long years after his retirement to pursue a claim for the grant of selection 
grade. This was clearly beyond the residuary period of limitation of three years provided in Article 37 of 
the Schedule to the Limitation Act, /963, That apart, in the decision of this Court in State of Rajasthan v. 
Shankar Lal Parmar, the Court has considered the ambit of the scheme for selection grade. This Court has 
held thus: 


[भाग ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जनवरी 3, 2026/पौष 3, 947 7669 


"6. Another important and relevant clause in the said order for our perusal is Clause 7, which is also 
reproduced hereinbelow: 


7. Selection grades in terms of this order shall be granted only to those employees whose record of service is 
satisfactory. The record of service which makes one eligible for promotion on the basis of seniority shall be 
considered to be satisfactory for the purpose of grant of the selection grade. 


7. Clause 7 makes it clear that only those employees would be entitled for grant of selection grades, whose 
service record has been satisfactory and are otherwise eligible for promotion on the basis of seniority but 
have not been able to get the same as there might not be any channel of promotion or for want of sanctioned 
posts in the cadre." 


&. The Court held that in terms of Clause 7, only those employees whose service record has been satisfactory 
could be entitled to be granted selection grade. In this context, the Court held: 


77, Clause 7 further makes it clear that only those/such employees would be entitled to be granted selection 
grade whose service record has been satisfactory. This implicitly shows that the person who has an 
untainted, unblemished, clean and unpolluted record in service would be treated on a higher pedestal than 
those who have either tainted, blemished, unclean or polluted record. This obviously appears to be a 
reasonable classification and is under the ambit and touchstone of Article 74 of the Constitution. There is 
neither any ambiguity nor any doubt in the same." 


9. On the touchstone of the above principles, it is evident that the respondent had been subjected to several 
disciplinary proceedings and as many as 79 charge- sheets were issued against him which resulted in 
penalties of a varying nature. The service record of the respondent cannot be regarded as untainted or clean. 


409. Ms Nidhi, learned counsel for the respondent submitted that some of the penalties which were imposed 
on the respondent were without cumulative effect. The consequence of the withholding of increments without 
cumulative effect is that after the period prescribed, the respondent would be entitled to restoration of the 
original pay scale or the original pay. However, this does not obviate the position that the imposition of the 
penalty itself indicates that the service record of the employee was not satisfactory. Another submission 
which has been urged is that the penalties were of a minor nature. Assuming that to be so, it is evident that 
for the grant of selection grade, the respondent did not fulfil the requirements of a clean record of service. 
The grant of the selection grade is not a matter of right and was subject to the terms and conditions which 
were stipulated. The respondent failed to fulfil these terms and conditions. 


I]. For the above reasons, we are of the view that both on the question of limitation as well as on merits, the 
respondent was not entitled to the relief which was sought. The suit instituted by the respondent resp seven 
years after he had demitted office was barred by limitation. That apart, the respondent failed to meet the 
basic requirements for the selection grade." (Emphasis supplied) 


Further, the Hon'ble Supreme Court, in Bichitrananda Behera (supra), after considering the erstwhile 
judgments of Union of India v. Tarsem Singh, Union of India v. N. Murugesan, and Chairman, State Bank of 
India v. M.J. James, concluded that delay and laches are vital in service matters, and can be seen as 
acquiescence." 


AWARD 


For the foregoing reasons the workman is not entitled for any relief as per the Reference No. L-40i2/27/202-IR(B- 
I) dated 30.08.202 and the same is answered accordingly. 


Let two copies of this award be sent to the Ministry for publication. 
Date: 5.07.2025 
Justice ANIL KUMAR, Presiding Officer

.On receipt of the reference, notice was issued to the workman for 20.2.2024 for appearance and for filing

presumed that the service is complete. However, today also no one has turned up on behalf of workman 

despite of service. The workman has been given sufficient opportunities for appearance and for filing claim 
statement, but he did not turned up in spite of the opportunities afforded to him for filing claim statement, 
which shows that he is not interested in adjudication of the matter on merit. 

2.In such circumstances, this Tribunal is left with no choice, except to pass a 'No Claim Award'. Accordingly, 

"No Claim Award' is passed in the present reference.

Central Government vide Notification No.L-!40/7/2008-IR(DU) dated 20.05.2009, under sub-section () 
and sub-section (2A) of Section 0 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (hereinafter called the Act), has referred the 
following Industrial dispute for adjudication to this Tribunal: - 


“Whether the demand of S/Shri Jarnail Singh S/o Banwari Lal, (ii) Bhim Singh S/o Mangata Ram, (iii) 
Suresh Kumar S/o Mam Chand, (iv) Jagdish S/o Babu Ram, (v) Ram Singh S/o Prakash Chand, (vi) 
Amar Singh S/o Pannu Ram, for engagement in the Military Farm, Bir Dhantauri/Ambala is just and 
fair? If yes, what relief the concerned are entitled to and from which date?” 


l. The brief facts, related to the case are that the workmen were employed with respondent no.2 
w.e.f. 07.04.990 as farms hands. All of them were sponsored by the employment exchange against the requisition 
sent by the respondent no.2 and after proper screening and scrutiny by a duly constituted committee were appointed 
on daily wage basis. The workmen were also granted temporary status under the Scheme dated 0!.09.993 of the 
Government of India. The workmen were governed by the Model Standing Orders issued by the respondents under 
the Industrial Employment (Standing Orders) Act, 946 in 982. Under these statutory orders, the workman engaged 
through employment exchange, who has been in continuous service for 6 months was to be brought on to the regular 
establishment. It was also a condition that no recruitment is to be made otherwise that through the employment 
exchange by issuing executive instructions from time to time. The seniority of all workmen is to be on all India Basis. 
It is also policy that surplus are to be transferred and adjusted at other stations and not to be retrenched. The work is 
of permanent and perennial nature and includes agriculture and other diary functions. Vide order dated 9.03.999, 
services of all the workmen were retrenched on the ground of reduction in workload with a specific assurance that 
they would be considered for re-engagement as per their seniority as and when regular vacancy occurs. They may 
also take up job basis work to the extent available. On 28.07.999, the farm was stated to have been closed due to 
some administrative decisions, which are not on record. However, immediately thereafter the farm was revived. The 
disbanding and closing of the farm was only a sham transaction to dispense with the services of the workmen. 


[भाग [IVS 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जनवरी 3, 2026/07 3, 947 7673 


2. The applicants filed OA No.l38-HR-92 before Hon'ble Central Administrative Tribunal, Chandigarh 
(hereinafter called as Hon'ble CAT, Chandigarh) and Hon'ble CAT, Chandigarh vide order dated 23.09.992 give 
directions to the respondent to maintain status quo qua the present applicants and the said order was modified vide 
order dated ३].05.993 with the direction to the respondent not to terminate services of the applicants as long as 
requirement of work is there. Thereafter, vide order dated 28.07.999 (Ex. ४४५७४।/6), the Hon'ble Tribunal dispose of 
the aforesaid OA No.!38-HR-92 with the direction that party would be bound by the observation and direction given 
in this case not to terminate the services of the workmen. The workmen filed CWP ]१२०.6420 of 999 before the 
Hon'ble Punjab & Haryana High Court against the decision dated 28.07.999 (Ex. ४४७॥/6) of Hon'ble CAT, 
Chandigarh. The said CWP was dismissed on 30.08.2006 by the Hon'ble High Court. The management in its written 
statement filed before the Hon'ble High Court had stated that the Dhantauri Farm had been revived in the year 200]. 
It was therefore imperative on them to have offered the job to the workmen as required under Section 25H of the Act. 
In view of the order of retrenchment dated 9.03.!999, the management was under an obligation to re-engage/re- 
appoint them on the revival of the farm. It is prayed that the workmen be reinstated in service from the date new 
hands have been employed/engaged. 


35 Notice of claim was given to the respondent no.l & 2, who filed reply thereof maintaining therein that the 
workmen were retrenched in the year 999 due to non availability of work. The job in which the workmen were 
employed was not a regular nature and they were not engaged against any regular post with the military department. 
They worked as daily wager against casual work, which does not come within the purview of the Act. It is also 
maintained that the respondent no.l and 2 does not fall within the definition of ‘Industry’ as defined under the Act. 
Due to reduction of work at military farm at Bir Dhantauri, the workmen were retrenched after paying due 
compensation admissible in the rules. The seniority of casual labour is counted in farm enrolment and not on All 
India Basis as per rules. There is no existence of military farm at Bir Dhantauri at present. The said unit has been 
disbanded and remains closed w.e.f. 0!.04.2005. The land/assets of military farm at Bir Dhantauri have been 
transferred to local military authority, Station HQ Ambala Cantt. and army guard has already been deployed by 40 
Arty Division at Bir Dhantauri. As per orders of the higher authorities, the military department has been permitted to 
rear cattle in order to facilitate watch and ward of land/assets to avoid any encroachment and to avoid loss to the state 
till land and assets are not acquired by LMA. The routine work of rearing young stock is being done by outsourcing 
as per Government Policy on part of Military Farm, Ambala till permitted by the LMA. The Hon'ble Supreme Court 
has held that Government offices/state offices are not covered under the category of Industrial Establishments and as 
such labour court has no jurisdiction. The claim of applicants has already been dismissed by the Hon'ble CAT, 
Chandigarh and same order has been upheld by the Hon'ble High Court of Punjab & Haryana. It is prayed that the 
claim of applicants/workmen be dismissed. 


4. Parties were given opportunities to lead evidence. 
Evidence of workman:

has been cross-examined by the AR of respondent no.! & 2. AR for workman closed the evidence on behalf of

Submissions of applicants:- 


8. While arguing the case, ld. counsel for the applicants contended that applicants were employed by 
respondent no.2 w.e.f. 07.04.990 as farm hand through employment exchange after appropriate screening committee 
on daily wage basis. Workmen worked as such and they filed case before Hon'ble CAT, Chandigarh (OA |२०.।38- 
HR-92) as they were apprehending their termination by the respondent no.2. On 23.09.992, Hon'ble CAT 
Chandigarh ordered that status quo be maintained by the respondents and workmen may not be terminated. However, 
their request for regularization was not accepted as per order dated 28.07.999 (5%.४४५७४|/6). Against the order of the 
Hon'ble CAT, Chandigarh, workmen filed CWP No.!6420 of 999 before the Hon'ble Punjab & Haryana High. 
However, Hon'ble High Court dismissed the said CWP on 30.08.2006. In the meanwhile, workmen were removed 
from the services vide letter dated 9.03.999 (Annexure B only of one workman placed on record and remaining 
letters are alike Annexure B) by maintaining that they are being removed from the services due to reduction of work 
and in the retrenchment order of each workman, it was stated as follows:- 


“2. You would be considered for re-engagement as per your seniority as and when regular vacancy occurs. You may 
also take up “Job Basis” work to the extent available.” 


9. AR for workmen further contended that after the dismissal of the CWP, it came to the knowledge of the 
workmen that junior to the applicants were also retrenched on 9.03.999/23.03.999. He further contended that 
workmen were senior to the said workmen namely Ramcharan and |8 others. The said Junior of the workmen Sh. 
Ramcharan and 8 others filed a claim statement on !3.05.200 and in the said case, CGIT-I, Chandigarh passed 
order in view of the settlement dated 25.08.200 (Annexure C) done by the respondent no. | & 2 with those juniors. 
In this way, juniors to the workmen continued to work with respondents. However, workmen were not called for any 
work. He further contended that in the year 20l7, juniors to the workmen apprehend that they were likely to be 
retrenched, they filed OA No.060/l!29/207 before the Hon'ble CAT Chandigarh and Hon'ble CAT Chandigarh vide 
order dated 8.02.20l9 (Annexure E) directed to the respondent to constitute a committee and ordered their 
continuation in the service. However, later on, nothing was done by the respondents and therefore execution was filed 
by the said juniors in MA No.952 of 20I9 in the Hon'ble CAT, Chandigarh (Annexure F), in which it is held as 
under:- 


24. After going through the detailed objections filed by the respondents and the subsequent rebuttal to the 
same, we feel that the order dated 76.02.2079 passed in OA /४०.0600429/204/ has not been complied with in 
its letter and spirit in the light of record of applicants, orders passed by respondents relating to age and grant 
of Temporary Status and in the interest of justice, equity and fair-play. 


[भाग [Ive 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जनवरी 3, 2026/07 3, 947 7675 


22.In view of the forgoing, all these Executions Applications are allowed. Respondents are directed for strict 
compliance of the order dated /6,92.2049 in its true letter and spirit within two months of the date of receipt of 
this order. 


0. Thereafter, respondents had gone in writ petition bearing CWP No.22648 of 2025 against the said order and 
Hon'ble High Court in its decision dated 3.08.2025 (Annexure G) has held that directions given by Hon'ble CAT 
Chandigarh on 2.03.2025 (Annexure F) to implement order dated |8.02.20I9 (Annexure E) be complied with and 
has dismissed the appeal of Union of India. He thus contended that in this case, present workmen, who were senior as 
per seniority list (Ex.WW/4) were entitled to be kept in services, however, it has not been done, which is violation of 
Section 25H and 25T of the Act. Thus, prayed for reinstatement of the present workmen from the date when juniors 
were kept in service and for payment of all consequential benefits. 


Il. On the other hand, Id. counsel for the management contended that compensation was paid to the applicants 
along with other workers at the time of their retrenchment. The applicants were purely daily wagers and now the 
military farm stands closed. Hence, the applicants are not entitled for their continuation in the service and cannot be 
granted any relief. 


2. I have given due consideration to the arguments advanced by AR for both the parties. 


3. Before entering into the actual controversy between the parties, it will be pertinent to mention those facts 
which are admitted between the parties. It has been admitted by both the parties that currently Bir Dhantauri Military 
Farm stands closed. Under these circumstances, we are to see if applicants are workmen and respondent is an industry 
and if so, whether workmen are entitled for reinstatement from the date when their juniors were kept in service and 
all consequential benefits or otherwise what relief can be given to them. 


4. Now first question is whether the applicants are workman. In this regard, it is pertinent to mention here that 
onus to prove that workman are in the employment of respondent is always on the workmen/claimants and it is for the 
workmen to adduce evidence to prove the factum of his employment with the respondent. There is hardly any dispute 
with the preposition of law as propounded by the Hon'ble Supreme Court in several cases. In the present case, it has 
clearly been admitted by the respondents that applicants were appointed w.e.f. 07.04.990 as farm hand (daily wager) 
and they were retrenched in the year !999 and all the workmen were appointed through employment exchange. As 
such, it clearly establishes the relationship of employee and employer between the applicants and respondents. In this 
regard, reference can be made to the decision in the case of Devinder Singh Versus Municipal Council, Sanaur, 
AIR 20// Supreme Court 2532, wherein the Hon'ble Apex Court while interpreting the provisions of Section 2(S) of 
Act which deals with the definition of “workman” has observed as follows:- 


“The source of employment, the quantum of recruitment, the terms & conditions of employment/contract of 
service, the quantum of wages/pay and mode of payment are not at all relevant for deciding whether or not a 
person is a workman within the meaning of Section 2(s) of the Act. The definition of workman also does not 
make any distinction between full time and part time employee or a person appointed on contract basis. There 
is nothing in the plain language of Section 2(s) from which it can be inferred that only person employed on 
regular basis or a person employed for doing whole time job is a workman and the one employed on 
temporary, part time or contract basis on fixed wages or as a casual employee or for doing duty for fixed hours 
is not a workman.” 


5. It is clear from the perusal of aforesaid observation that even if a person is engaged on temporary, part time 
daily wages or contract basis or for doing any other kind of work and is duly paid wages for the said work, in that 
eventuality such a person would be covered by the definition of “workman” as provided under Section 2(s) of the 
Act. Thus, it is held that workmen were engaged on temporary basis. However, they fall under the definition of 
workman as stated above under Section 2(s) of the Act. 


6. The question which arises for consideration is whether the establishment Military Farm is an industry. The 
Hon'ble Apex Court in the case of Banglore_ Water Supply & Sewerage Board Vs. A. Rajappa /9780(36) FLR 266 
dealt at length with the ambit and scope of expression "industry" as defined in Section 2(J) of the Act, and has laid 
down triple test for determining whether a particular establishment is industry or not. The triple test is where (a) 
systematic activity (b) organized by cooperation between employer and employee(the direct and substantial element 
is commercial (c) for the production or distribution of goods and services to calculated to satisfy human wants and 
wishes prima facie is an "Industry" in that enterprise. Coming into the case in hand, the work assigned to the 
workmen relates to do work of catering animals and other related work for production of milk with the cooperation of 
the workmen and respondent. Thus, all the necessary ingredients of the above judgment are satisfied. 


7. It is also mentioned here that workmen were not regular employees of the respondent as regular employees 
are governed by Central Civil Service Rules. The workmen, who were casual workers, are not governed by the 
aforesaid rules and are governed by the Act and if any dispute arises, the same is governed by the Act. 


8. Further, Hon'ble Supreme Court in case titled as Chief Conservator of Forest Versus Jagannath Maruti 
Kondhare (7००6 (2) SCT 764), has held that work, which was not be regarded as a part of inalienable of inescapable 


7676 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JANUARY 3, 2026/PAUSHA 3, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


function of the State and which work can be well undertaken by a private agency does not require an instrumentality 
of State, such functions of the State fall within the ambit of Industry. Thus, function of doing diary cannot be said to 
be sovereign function of the State and thus for this purpose of industrial dispute, respondents would be termed as 
industry. 


9. Now next question, which arise for consideration is that whether workmen who were retrenched vide order 
dated 9.03.999 (Annexure B) and their juniors were also retrenched on the same date, they have not been offered 
employment in view of the Section 25H of the Act, which read as follows:- 


25H. Re-employment of retrenched workmen.—Where any workmen are retrenched, and the employer proposes 
to take into his employ any persons, he shall, in such manner as may be prescribed, give an opportunity 2 [to 
the retrenched workmen who are citizens of India to offer themselves for reemployment and such retrenched 
workman] who offer themselves for re-employment shall have preference over other persons. 


20. Admittedly, in this case, workmen were removed vide retrenchment order dated 9.03.999. One of the 
order has been placed on file (Annexure B) by the AR for workmen, operative part of which is reproduced as under:- 


I. Due to reduction of work, your casual employment is hereby terminated with immediate effect. One month 
salary in lieu of notice and compensation 873 days salary at current rate for each completed year with 240 
days attendance is enclosed vide cheque 0, /79972 dated 23.93, ॥/999 for 77. /4692. 00. 


2. You would be considered for re-engagement as per your seniority as and when regular vacancy occurs. You 
may also take up “Job Basis” work to the extent available. 


2]. In the retrenchment order it is mentioned that the workmen would be considered as and when regular 
vacancy occur, respondent were required to engage them. 


However, in this case, junior to the workmen were retained in service and present workmen were senior to the said 
workmen as is clear from the seniority list placed on file by the applicants as 5.४७ ]/4. Name of the present 
workmen are at Sr. No.l0 to !2 and 4 to l6 and name of junior to the present workmen is at Sr. No.l7 to 9, 2] to 
24, 26 to 3l, 32 & 35 as per the said seniority list (:.४४५७॥/4). The same is also been highlighted by the applicant in 
Annexure P-2 and P-3 (part of +5.४४७॥/4). 


22, It is further mentioned here that junior of the applicant were also removed in the month of March 999 and 
the said junior to the workmen, who were 49 in number, filed a claim statement on 3.05.990 in the CGIT-I, 
Chandigarh and the matter was settled there on 25.08.20I0 (Annexure C). Relevant part of the order is as follows: 


“Case taken up in Lok Adalat for conciliation. It is agreed upon that all the /9 workers whose name figured in 
the list supplied by the Ministry with reference shall visit the office of the prescribed authority, Shri Pradeep 
Bahuguna at about 79.30 AM on 26.08.20]0. It is further agreed that the prescribed authority shall make an 
endeavor to settle all the claim of the persons whose name figured in the list as per the availability and 
requirement as early as possible. The file shall be kept pending for the next Lok Adalat for further discussion 
in Lok Adalat through conciliation proceedings. List case on /35,09,2070 in Lok Adalat.” 


23. Thus, in this way, junior to the present applicants continued with the work, however seniors (present 
applicants) were retrenched and were not called for any work. In the year 207, juniors apprehended that they would 
be retrenched, they filed OA No.l222 of 20I7 and OA No.Ili5 of 207 before CAT, Chandigarh and CAT, 
Chandigarh vide order dated 8.02.209 (Annexure E) directed the respondents to constitute a committee within a 
period of three weeks, relevant para of which is reproduced as under: 


Il. Accordingly, the OAs are disposed of by directing the respondents to constitute a committee within a 
period of three weeks from the date of receipt of a certified copy of this order, which will conduct and 
conclude its proceedings within two months thereafter. If applicants wish to submit anything before 
committee, the committee may also consider their pleas, before passing the final order. 


24. Later on, nothing was done by the respondent, Thereafter, execution was filed by the aforesaid juniors in 
MA No.954 of 209 in OA No.I222 of 20!7 and MA No.956 of 20I9 in OA No.I5 of 207, which were allowed 
vide order dated 2.03.2025 (Annexure F) directing the respondents to strict compliance of order dated 8.02.209 
(Annexure E) in true letter and spirit within two months of the date of receipt of that order. Relevant part of order 
dated 2.03.2025 (Annexure F) passed in MA No.954/956 of 209 is reproduced as under: - 


“2L.After going through the detailed objections filed by the respondents and the subsequent rebuttal to the 
same, we feel that the order dated /6.92.2079 passed in OA ४०.०69/729/2047 has not been complied with in 
its letter and spirit in the light of record of applicants, order passed by respondents relating to age and grant 
of Temporary Status and in the interest of justice, equity and fair-play. 


22.In view of the foregoing, all these Execution Applications are allowed. Respondents are directed for strict 
compliance of the order dated /6,.02.29079 in its true letter and spirit within two months of the date of receipt 
of this order.” 


[भाग [Ive 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जनवरी 3, 2026/पौष 3, 947 7677 


25. Thereafter, respondent had gone in writ petition bearing CWP No.22648 of 2025 and Hon'ble High Court in 
its decision has held that direction given by the CAT Chandigarh vide order dated 2.03.2025 (Annexure F) to 
implement the order dated 8.02.209 (Annexure E) and appeal of the respondent was dismissed. In this way, junior 
to the applicant continued in service, whereas applicants were retrenched, who were senior as per seniority list 
(-5.४४५७।/4), were entitled to be kept in service, which is clear violation of Section 25T and Section 25H of the Act. 
Section 25T of the Act provides as under:- 


“25T. Prohibition of unfair labour practice.—No employer or workman or a trade union, whether registered under 
the Trade Unions Act, /926 (76 of /926), or not, shall commit any unfair labour practice.” 


26. It is also added here that applicants had filed OA No.l38-HR-92 seeking their regularization before the 
Hon'ble CAT, Chandigarh and vide order dated 28.07.!999 (Ex. ४४/५७४७।/6), the OA was dismissed. However, they 
remained in service due to status quo given vide order dated 23.09.992. However, due to dismissal of the OA vide 
order dated 28.07.999 (४5.0४५७|/6), they were removed from service on the same date on 28.07.999. Even CWP 
filed against the order dated 28.07.999 (Ex. ४४५७४|/6) was dismissed vide order dated 30.08.2006. However, main 
question involved in the said litigation was regarding the regularization. However, in the present case, applicants have 
sought relief of unfair labour practice under Section 25T and retaining of juniors under Section 25H of the Act. 


2s Thus, in view of the above, workmen were entitled for continuity in service as and when vacancy arises. 
However, this fact can also not be ignored that junior to the workmen were retained in service ignoring applicant. At 
this stage, Bir Dhanturi Military Farm does not exist. Hence, only remedy left with this Tribunal is to grant them 
compensation. 


28. Keeping in view the facts and circumstances of the case, each applicant is entitled for Rs.50,000/- along with 
interest @6% from the date of reference of this case till the payment is made. 


29. Let copy of this award be sent to the Appropriate Government as required under Section 7 of the Act for 
publication. 


KAMAL KANT, Presidng Officer

जता, The management assured to give wages to all workers in the first week of month. 


XXIV. Whether the action The Chief Executive Officer, M/s Rattle Hydro Power Corporation Shalimar, 
Kishtwar and The Chief General Manager, M/s Megha Infrastructure Engineering Limited, Rattle HE 
Project, Darabshalla, District Kishtwar, UT of Jammu and Kashmir for not giving winter allowance 
@ Rs.2000/- per year to all its workers is legal and justified? If not, then to what relief the concerned 
workmen are entitled to and from which date?” 


l. On receipt of the reference, notice was issued to the workman for 20.2.2024 for appearance and for 
filing claim statement. However, no one turned up on behalf of workman on 20.[2.2024 and fresh notice was 
issued to the workman on 20.2.2024 for 04.03.2025. However, n notice sent to workman received back 
unserved with remark ‘insufficient address’ and fresh notice was issued on 04.03.2025 for 07.03.2025. As per 
track report, notice was duly served on the address of workman on 09.05.2025. However, no one turned up on 
behalf of workman on 03.07.2025 also and fresh notice was again issued for 27.0.2025 for appearance and 
for filing claim statement, which was duly served on the address on 20.09.2025. However, today also no one 
has turned up on behalf of workman despite of service. The workman has been given sufficient opportunities 
for appearance and for filing claim statement, but he did not turned up in spite of the opportunities afforded to 
him for filing claim statement, which shows that he is not interested in adjudication of the matter on merit. 


2.In such circumstances, this Tribunal is left with no choice, except to pass a 'No Claim Award'. Accordingly, 
"No Claim Award' is passed in the present reference. 


3.Let copy of this award be sent to the Appropriate Government as required under Section !7 of the Act for 
publication. 


KAMAL KANT, Presiding Officer 
नई दिल्‍ली, 24 दिसम्बर, 2025

l. Sh. Daleep Singh (hereinafter called as workman) has directly filed the claim statement against the 
management under sub-section (2) and (3) of Section 2A of the Industrial Disputes Act, !947 (hereinafter 
called as the Act) claiming quashing of termination order dated 09.05.20l4 (Ex.W2) and his reinstatement in 
service w.e.f. 6.06.200 along with full back wages and all consequential benefits. 


2; It is added here during the present proceedings, workman Sh. Daleep Singh had expired on 09.0.202 and his 
legal heirs were allowed to be impleaded as party vide order dated 22..2022. 


3. It is maintained in the claim statement that workman was appointed as Beldar in Salal H.E. Project and joined 
the service on 02.09.984 and thereafter remained posted in Chamera Power Station-l, Khairi, Chamera Power 
Station-II, Karian and since year 2005, he was working under Chamera Power Station III, Dharwala. 


4. Management of Chamera Power Station-III has illegally terminated the services of workman by way of struck 
off his name from the roll of NHPC w.e.f. 6.06.200 vide order dated 09.05.20I4 (Ex.W2) on the ground that 
he is deemed to have left the service of the company of his own accord w.e.f. 6.06.20I0 in terms of Rule 
3.2.7 of NHPC Leaves Rules. The termination order dated 09.05.20I4 (Ex.W2), was received in his home on 
9.06.204. 


5. Against the illegal termination, workman filed Industrial Dispute in the office of Assistant Labour 
Commissioner (Central)-Cum-Conciliation Officer, Chandigarh (hereinafter called as ALC, Chandigarh) vide 
Demand Notice Dated 6.09.20l4 (Ex.W3) on which conciliation proceedings were held but no settlement 
could be arrived at and mandatory period of 45 days of filing the dispute before conciliation officer as 
provided under Section 2A(2) of the Act has been completed on 6.!.204 and certificate to this effect has 
been issued by the ALC Chandigarh on 6.2.204.

It is also maintained that absence of workman from duty is not intentional but was under compelling 
circumstances which was beyond the control of workman as he was suffering with Schizophrenia disease since 
long time and was getting medical treatment from Rajpal Hospital Neuro-Psychiatric and Addiction Hospital, 
Pathankot from 5.06.200 to 3.05.204, which is evident from medical certificate dated 3.05.20l4 (Ex.W4) 
issued by the Treating Doctor Kanwar Rajpal Singh Neuro Psychiatrist. 


Prior to 5.06.200, he was getting treatment of same disease from the said Doctor from the year 2008 and 
onward. On 5.06.20l0 when he was on duty, he felt seriously ill and then family Members shifted him to 
Pathankot for seeking medical treatment and he remained under prolonged medical treatment till 3.05.204. 


In the second/third week of April 204, there was some slight improvement in his health, then he along-with 
his wife went to HR wing of Chamera Power Station-II and approached to the then Senior Manager (HR) to 
accept his joining report but the senior manager (HR) told him that the management is not in a position to 
accept his joining report as matter has been referred to Corporate Office and senior manager (HR) advised him 
and his wife to sent his representation to the Director (Personnel) and if he so desired to meet him in person in 
this regard. Accordingly the workman made one representation (Annexure P-5) to the Director (Personnel) and 
the copy of the same was endorsed to the Executive Director (HR) NHPC Ltd. which was sent through Fax by 
HR Wing of the Power Station and on 22.04.20l4 and 06.05.204, the workman along with his wife and 
General Secretary of NHPC employees Union, Corporate Office met with General Manager (HR) and Director 
(Personnel) separately at Faridabad office and explained each about his genuine illness/disease and handed 
over a copy of aforesaid representation to them and also requested them to allow him to resume his duties but 
there no effect. He was simply told to go to the local project management and approach him for joining at his 
own level. 


On 4.05.20I4 (Ex.W6), the workman submitted his joining report to the Sr. Manager (HR), Chamera Power 
Station-II along with a copy of medical certificate dated 3.05.204 (Ex.W4) but he returned the same to the 
workman and told that since the department has issued termination order from service on 09.05.204 as such 
his joining report cannot be accepted. 


The workman also sent various telegrams (Ex.W7) dated 08.06.200, 20.I0.20l0, 5.02.20, 06.05.20], 
02.09.20I, 2.0.20I, 29.2.20I, 23.03.20I2, 2.05-20i2, 2.07.20l2 and thereafter his wife gave 
information to the then Sr. Manager (HR) on telephone every month about his ill health. 


Before passing termination order, management was to serve charge sheet upon the workman and hold inquiry 
but in the present case neither management served any charge sheet nor held any enquiry. No opportunity of 
personal hearing was afforded to the workman before passing termination order dated 09.05.204 (Ex.W2). 


There is a settled law that illegal termination of service amount to retrenchment and management have to 
comply with the provisions of Section 25-F of the Act but in the present case Management has not issued one 
month notice in writing nor paid one month wages in lieu of notice period and has also not been paid any 
retrenchment compensation to the workman. 


Management has not been adhered the procedure laid down in Clause 34 of Certified Standing Orders before 
passing final order of termination of services. Because the services of the workman is governed by Certified 
Standing Orders of the Chamera Power Station-II/Chamera H.E, Project Stage-IHI, as Such the action of 
management in terminating his services is illegal and clear cut violation of Certified Standing Orders and 
instructions issued by the Corporate office vide letter dated 2.09.993 (5.४४।2). 


The action of management in striking of the name of the workman from the role of the corporation w.e-f. 
6.06.20i0 vide order dated 09.05.204 (Ex.W2) on the ground of unauthorized absence from duty is also 
arbitrary and unjustified. The workman is unemployed and nowhere gainfully employed since the date of his 
termination and having no other source of income. The workman made a representation dated 09.07.204 
(Ex.W8) to the management with the request to review order dated 09.05.204 (Ex.W2), but the same was 
rejected by the Manager (M), Incharge HR by way of passing non speaking order dated 2.08.204 (Ex.W9). It 
is maintained that the action of management in terminating the service is illegal and against the principle of 
natural justice and it is prayed that the termination order dated 09.05.20I4 (Ex.W2) be quashed and workman 
be reinstated in service w.e.f. 6.06.20l0 along with full back wages and all consequential benefits. 


Notice of the claim petition was given to the management, who filed reply thereof maintaining therein that the 
workman is a habitual absentee and has remained absent from duties unauthorizedly as specified in the notice. 
He was absent from the duty continuously w.e.f. l6.06.20l0. The workman did not respond to any of the 
notices issued from time to time and explain the circumstances with proof about the absence nor he cared to 
join duty. For this long period of absence his name was struck off from the rolls of the corporation as he was 
deemed to have left the services of the company on his own accord. As such strucking off name of the 
workman from the roll is not termination as alleged by him, therefore, it is not an industrial dispute. The 
workman did not resume his duties, which is the prime responsibility of the bonafied employees of the 
corporation.

The workman was advised time and again by the management to submit his prescriptions of treatment so that 
the period of absence without permission may be considered for regularization within rules. In this connection, 
various correspondences were made with the workman vide various letters dated 25.06.20l! (Ex.M-3), 
2.09.20I] (Ex.M-l) and 3.2.20I (Ex.M-2) to submit the documentary proof. These letters addressed to 
the workman were returned back with remarks that no one was available at home of the workman to receive 
the letter. The workman kept on sending telegrams that he is not well and he will resume duties after fitness. 
Since the communications sent to the workman at his last known address were received bank unserved, the 
management gave public notice through publication in two regional newspapers both Hindi and English on 
.07.202 (Ex.M-4) with the direction to resume duty within 07 days of publication notice i.e. by 8.07.20i2 
but the workman failed to resume duty. Thereafter, again public notice in local and national newspapers, both 
in Hindi and English were published on 0.02.204 and also on 07.04.20I4 (Ex.M-5) directing him to report 
for duty by 22.04.204, failing which he shall be deemed to have left the services of the management on his 
own accord and his name will be struck off from the roll of NHPC from the date of his absence in accordance 
with Rule 3.2.7 of NHPC Leave Rules. Despite several efforts made by the management, the workman failed 
to respond to the either of the publications. 


The workman submitted a medical certificate issued on 3.05.20I4 (Ex.W4) by the doctor namely Dr. Kanwar 
Rajpal Singh, Neuro Psychiatric, Pathankot without any supporting documents like prescription etc. after the 
long span of 4 years of absence, which shows it is totally fabricated documentary evidence with ulterior 
motive. Mere certificate is not sufficient to prove that he was suffering from prolonged illness of 
Schizophrenia disease as workman has not submitted any prescription/documents regarding treatment since 
200 to 204 for the reasons best known to the workman. In absence of any documents, it is impossible to 
presume that the workman is suffering from any illness or undergoing treatment. Non submission of 
documentary proof by the workman is violation of Rules and the Certified Standing Orders of Chamera-III 
Power Station. It is admitted that Standing Orders, which have been certified under the Industrial Employment 
(Standing Orders) Act, 946 become part of the statutory terms and conditions of service between the 
employer and employee govern the relationship between the parties. But there is no provision either in this 
statute or in the Act which states that an order of dismissal is illegal if it is not preceded by a proper and valid 
domestic enquiry. The workman has fabricated the story, which is not sustainable in the eyes of law. It is 
maintained that the claim of the workman is baseless, without any merit and deserves to be dismissed 
with cost.

Submissions of Applicants:- 


20. 


While arguing the case, AR for workman contended that the workman has joined as Beldar in Salal Hydro 
Electric Project under the respondent and lastly was working under respondent no.l at Chamera and was 
removed from service illegally on vide order dated 09.05.20I4 (Ex.W2). It is further stated that workman was 
getting treatment of Schizophrenia from Dr. Rajpal from 5.06.200 till 3.05.20l4 as OPD patient as per 
medical certificate dated 3.05.20l4 (Ex.W4) issued by the said doctor. It is also stated that the workman has 
sent various telegrams 08.06.200, 20.0.200, 5.02.20, 06.05.20I, 0.09.20], 2.0.20, 29.2.20I, 
23.03.20I2, 2.05-202, 2.07.202, receipts thereof are Ex.W/7 to the respondent. The workman was under 
acute mental depression as such he cannot be said to be remained unauthorized absent. Therefore, respondent 
without holding any inquiry, terminated the services of the workman vide order dated 09.05.204 (Ex.W2). He 
further contended that workman is governed by the certified standing orders (Ex.WIl). As per clause 32 
provides minor and major penalty, which could have been intricate on the workman after holding domestic 
inquiry as per law. Clause 34 of the said standing order provides procedure how disciplinary action is to be 
taken against the workman the detailed procedure has been prescribed under Clause 34. As per said clause 34, 
no minor or major penalty can be imposed without holding any regular inquiry. For that, regular inquiry is 
necessitated, which was not done in this. Moreover, as per Ex.W2 issued by the project head, even if 
workman remains absent, he could not have been removed from service without holding inquiry and thus order 
is liable to be set aside. 


Submissions of respondents: 


2I. 


22. 


23. 


On the other hand, AR for respondent contended that workman become absent unauthorizedly and 
continuously w.e.f. l6.06.20l0. The workman was asked to submit documentary proof vide Ex.M-! dated 
2.09.20l, Ex.M-2 dated 3.2.20I and Ex.M-3 dated 25.06.20l and thereafter public notice was also 
given to the workman vide Ex.M-4 dated !.07.20I2 and Ex.M-5 dated 3.0.20I4 through publication in 
newspaper and thereafter his name was struck off as per Para 3.2.7 of Leave Rule (Ex.M7), which provides as 
follows:- 


3.2./7. If an employee willfully absents himself from duty for more than ten consecutive 
days without any intimation, a notice shall be issued to him, directing him to report for 
duty forthwith and in any case not beyond eight days after the issue of the notice. If he does 
not resume duty within such period the employee shall be deemed to have left the service of 
the Corporation of his own accord and his name shall be struck off the rolls of the 
Corporation with effect from the date of such absence. 


The workman was untraceable during this period and was thus accordingly removed from service as per Rules. 
He thus contended that the claim petition be dismissed. 


I have given due consideration to the arguments advanced by AR for both the parties. 


7686 


24. 


23; 


26.

THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JANUARY 3, 2026/PAUSHA 3, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


It is added here that in this case, admittedly, workman had remained absent for the period 5.06.200 to 
3.05.20l4 and as per workman, he sent various telegrams (Ex.W7) (l0 in number) to the respondent. 
However, as per respondents, workman was asked to submit documentary proof vide Ex.M-! dated 
2.09.20l, Ex.M-2 dated 3.2.20I and Ex.M-3 dated 25.06.20 and thereafter public notice was also 
given to the workman vide Ex.M-4 dated !.07.20I2 and Ex.M-5 dated 3].0].204 through publication in 
newspaper and therefore his name was struck off as per Para 3.2.7 of Leave Rule (Ex.M7). In this regard, it is 
pertinent to mention here that so far as sending of letters Ex.M-! dated 2.09.20l, Ex.M-2 dated 3.2.20I] 
and Ex.M-3 dated 25.06.20ll are concerned, they have admittedly been received back by respondent as 
undelivered and admittedly all the telegrams have been received by the respondent and thus respondent was 
required to sent notices on the addresses from which various telegrams were received, which was admittedly 
not done by the respondent. The respondents have sent the notices on the last known address of the workman. 
Thus, in this way notice cannot of any help of the respondents. 


Moreover, it is not disputed that workman was governed by certified standing orders and in this case, name of 
the workman has been struck of from the role NHPC vide notice dated 09.05.204 (Ex.W2) without holding 
any regular inquiry. In this regard, clause 34 of standing orders is very material and relevant part of standing 
orders is reproduced as under:- 


34, Disciplinary Action:- 


No order imposing any of the penalties specified under S.O. No.32 shall be made unless the 
procedure detailed below is adhered to:- 


I) The workman/employee alleged to have committed the misconduct shall be served a 
chargesheet, clearly setting for the imputation of misconduct and circumstances appearing 
against him and requiring explanation. The charge sheet shall be issued by appointing 
authority or disciplinary authority or any other higher authority as authorized by the 
management. 


4) Management Shall appoint a person (called presenting officer) to appear before the Enquiry 
Officer and conduct the proceedings on behalf of the management, for presenting evidence, 
record, witness, examination of witnesses. 


6) 22 -- -- --- ee 


33) the inquiry officer shall on conclusion of the enquiry, submit his report in writing giving his 
findings with reasons thereof to the authority which appointed the inquiry officer and a copy of the 
proceedings is to be supplied to the concerned workman. 


ia es Ss = ex 
75) --- ने शा _ af 


It is added here that removal from service was a major penalty as per clause 32 of the certified standing orders 
and name of the workman has been struck of without holding any regular inquiry as per clause 34 of the 
certified standing orders and thus order dated 09.05.204 (Ex.W2) is liable to be set aside. 


So far as struck of the name of the workman as per para 3.2.[7 of Leave Rules Ex.M7 is concerned, in this 
regard, letter (Ex.Wl2) was issued by the respondent. In para no.3 of the १४]2, it is mentioned by the 
respondent that in view of various judgments of the Hon'ble Supreme Court, the certified standing orders have 
statutory force and principle of natural justice should be followed in every case while acting on these certified 
standing orders. Moreover, AR for respondents made following statement: - 


Stated that the NHPC Leave Rules are made by the Board of Directors in accordance with standing orders. 
They are not statutory in nature. 


Thus Leave Rule, 3.2.!7 having no force of law and cannot be relied upon and thus workman is liable to be 
reinstated into services when no domestic inquiry was held. Moreover, there was no compliance of the 
provisions of the Act prior to termination of services of the workman. 


In view of the discussions, absence of workman from 5.06.200 to 3.05.20l4 would be treated as leave of 
kind dues and the order dated 09.05.204 is set aside. The workman is entitled to reinstatement in service with

all consequential benefits. Since, the workman has expired on 09.0.202, his legal heirs would be entitled for 
all benefits.

[भाग [ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जनवरी 3, 2026/पौष 3, 947 7689 


(JUDGMENT) 
(Passed on this 6" day of October, 2025) 


As per letter dated /0/20I9 by the Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, the reference 
has been made to this Tribunal under Section-l0 of Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (in short the ‘Act’) as per 
Notification No. L-420/67/208 (IR(DU)) dt. /0/209. The dispute under reference relates to: 


“Whether the action on the part of the management of SECL, Central Workshop, Korba (Chhattisgarh) in 
inflicting harsh punishment upon Sh. Raja Ram Kashtriya, Ex-clerk Grade-II by removing from service instead of 
giving lesser punishment on absenteeism ground is appropriate and justified? If not, what relief, Sh. Raja Ram 
Kashtriya espoused by the Secretary, Bhartiya Vikas theka mazdoor union are entitled to?” 


After registering the case on the basis of reference, notices were issued to the parties. They appeared and 
filed their respective statement of claim in defense. 


The case of the workman is mainly that while he was working as Clerk Grade-II with the management and 
was posted in Laxman Project, he was issued a charge sheet with respect to his unauthorized absence in the year 997 
and was terminated from the services. He was reinstated by management allowing his appeal and was transferred to 
Central Workshop. He was issued another charge sheet on 3.07.999, with an allegation of unauthorized and wilful 
absence from work w.e.f., 02.06.999 till date of charge sheet. Infact, he could not report at work place for this period 
of '’” months due to illness of his father. He could not inform the management about his absence due to urgency, he 
filed a reply to the charge sheet admitting the charges and assured that he will not repeat it. The management 
terminated his service vide order dated 06.02.200, he came to know about this order only on 22.05.2004 when he got 
the notice of management to vacate the accommodation allotted to him by management as he was terminated from 
service. He raised the dispute. After failure of conciliation, dispute was referred to this Tribunal. 


According to the workman, infact, he had not admitted the charges rather he had explained his absence, 
which was wrongly taken by management as his admission to the charges. The management thus, acted arbitrarily 
treating his explanation as his admission of guilt and the punishment is also hash. 


The case of the management is mainly that the services of the workman were dismissed vide order dated 
06.02.200I. The present dispute has been raised in the year 209, there is an unexplained delay of l8 years since, the 
records are old, they are no traceable with the management, the workman was habitual absentee. He was awarded 
previous punishments also for his unauthorized absence. He was once dismissed for his unauthorized absence but was 
reinstated allowing his mercy petition but he did not improve and absented himself unauthorizedly, an enquiry was 
conducted with respect to the charges of misconduct by way of absenting himself unauthorizedly from 02.06.999 to 
3.07.999. After management received no satisfactory reply to the charge sheet in the enquiry, the workman 
participated, the enquiry officer submitted the enquiry report holding the charges proved. The workman was issued a 
show cause notice on the enquiry report and thereafter the termination order dated 06.02.200I was passed. 


The enquiry papers were never produced before this Tribunal. According to management, they have weeded 
out as per Rules. Before this Tribunal, the workman has filed his affidavit as his Examination-in-chief, he has been 
cross-examined by management. The management did not examined any witnesses before this Tribunal also in proof 
of the charges. 


I have heard argument of Learned Counsel for workman Mr. Pranay Choubey and Mr. Neeraj Kewat, 
Learned Counsel for Management. I have gone through the record as well. 


The facts which emerge from the evidence on record, that according to the management, an enquiry was 
conducted with respect to charges, the enquiry papers or any document with respect to enquiry have not been 
produced by management. 


Case of the workman is that, no intimation was sent to him with respect to enquiry. The management had 
an opportunity to prove the charges before this Tribunal. 


In his affidavit, the workman has stated that he communicated his apologies to management to the charges 
which were wrongly treated as admission and he was terminated on the said basis, he admits that earlier also he was 
terminated for the same misconduct but was reinstated as an act of mercy. 


The absence of workman is not denied by him. It is also not his case that he had informed the management 
about his absence or had applied for leave or had taken any permission from management in this respect. The absence 
is of about two months, the workman was required to at least inform the management about his absence stating the 
ground of his absence. Since, he did not inform the management, with respect to his absence and did not seek any 
regularization of his absence, his conduct is no doubt a misconduct by way of unauthorized absence. 


As regards the punishment, this is also not disputed that the workman has been punished earlier also for the 
same charges. Hence, since he is a habitual offender, the punishment of his removal cannot be held to be 
disproportionate to the charges. 


7690 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JANUARY 3, 2026/PAUSHA 3, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 
This is also established that the workman has raised dispute against termination of his services after l8 years, 
there is no explanation for this delay. In these circumstances, the workman is held entitled to no relief. 
On the basis of above discussion and findings, the reference deserves to be answered as follows:- 
AWARD 


Holding the action of the management of SECL, Central Workshop, Korba (Chhattisgarh) for dismissing 
Sh. Raja Ram Kashtriya, Ex-clerk Grade-II from services instead of giving lesser punishment on absenteeism 
ground, following Principles of Natural Justice is held to be legal and justified, he is entitled to no relief. 


No order as to cost. 
DATE:- 6-0-2025 
P.K. SRIVASTAVA, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 24 दिसम्बर, 2025

सरकार, रजिस्ट्रार, भारतीय प्रौद्योगिकी संस्थान, डीएवीवी परिसर, इंदौर, मेसर्स काप्रो मैनेजमेंट सॉल्यूशंस प्राइवेट

Management 


(JUDGMENT) 
(Passed on this 7 day of October - 2025) 


As per letter dated 03/02/2020 by the Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, the reference is 
made to this Tribunal under Section-!0 of Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (in short the ‘Act’)as per Notification No. J- 
(I-3)/2020-IR dt. 03/02/2020. The dispute under reference relates to: 


“Whether the action of the management of M/s.PrimeOne Workforce Pvt. Ltd. and M/s. Kaapro 
Management Solutions Pvt. Ltd. in terminating the services of workman Shri Balram Chouhan working 
in the establishment of Registrar, Indian Institute of Technology, Indore (M.P.) wef. 76.02.2076 and 
28.04.20I6 respectively, are just & proper? If yes, what relief the workman concerned is entitled to and 
from which date?” 


Thereafter, corrigendum dated 22.07.2022 was received containing amended reference which is as follows: 


"Whether the action of the management of the Registrar, Indian Institute of Technology(IIT), Indore 
(M.P.) / M/s.PrimeOne Pvt. Ltd. and M/s. Kaapro Management Solutions Pvt. Ltd. in terminating the services of 
workman Shri Balram Chouhan working in the establishment of Registrar, Indian Institute of Technology, Indore 
(M.P.) w.ef. 76.02.2078 and 28.04.20I6 respectively, are just and Proper? If yes, what relief the workman 
concerned is entitled to and from which date? 


Notices were issued to both the parties. They appeared and filed their respective statement of claim and 
defense. 


The workman appeared and filed his statement of claim with copy of his appointment order, pay slip, 
dismissal order. 


IIT also appeared through their counsel. But did not file any written statement of defense. 


The Outsourcing Agency M/s Kaapro Industries and M/s Prime One Workforce did not appear inspite of 
service of notice, hence reference proceeded ex-parte against them. 


In evidence, the workman filed his affidavit as his examination-in-chief. None appeared for cross- 
examination. He also filed photocopy of his appointment letter, pay slip and dismissal order. 


I have heard argument of Learned Counsel for workman Mr. Pranay Choubey. None appeared for 
Management. I have gone through the record as well. 


The amended reference is the issued for determination in the case in hand. 


Case of the Workman is mainly that, IIT has issued an advertisement for Drivers, cleaners etc. by way of 
walk in interview. He appeared and was selected for cleaner. It was nowhere provided in the advertisement that he 
will be appointed through a private contractor. There was no role of a private contractor or outsourcing agency in his 
recruitment process as it was done by the IIT themselves. He was shown as outsourced employee of contractors 
supplied by them to IIT which is incorrect in fact, and is a camouflage adopted by Management of IIT just to deprive 
him of his admissible rights and benefits under the law. . 


He was disengaged by the outsourcing company M/s Prime One Workforce on !5.02.20l8 without any 
notice or compensation which is against law. He has sought his reinstatement with back wages and benefits as an 
employee of IIT. 

In evidence, he has filed his affidavit as his examination in chief and also has filed photocopy of letter 


extending his work contract, experience certificates, and dismissal order. 


Though, he has corroborated his allegations in his statement of claim, but the documents filed by him speak 
against his case. Letter of extension of his service contract dated 0.2.204 is issued by the Outsourcing agency 
M/s Kaapro Management. It shows that his service contract was extended further from .2.204 to 0.09.20I5 on 


7692 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JANUARY 3, 2026/PAUSHA 3, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


previous conditions. Letter dated 27.04.207 is an another extension letter issued by M/s Prime One Workforce 
extending his work contract from 6.04.20I7 to 5.04.20I8 and letter dated 28.04.206 is the letter issued by 
Outsourcing Agencies M/s Kaapro Management Solutions disengaging him on expiry of his service contract. Letter 
dated 5.02.208 issued by M/s Prime One Workforce Outsourcing Agency has discontinued all his engagement by 
none extension of service contract. 


Hence, except his serving statement, there is nothing on record to show that the Workman was further 
employed by IIT and the Outsourcing Agency was a camouflage. It also comes out that, he was appointed by different 
agencies on contract basis and was discontinued on expiry of contract which is not a retrenchment as defined under 
Section 2(00) of the Act which is being reproduced as follows:- 


2 (00) "retrenchment" means the termination by the employer of the service of a workman for any reason 
whatsoever, otherwise than as a punishment inflicted by way of disciplinary action but does not include - 


(a) voluntary retirement of the workman; or 


(b) retirement of the workman on reaching the age of superannuation if the contract of employment 
between the employer and the workman concerned contains a stipulation in that behalf; or 


(bb) termination of the service of the workman as a result of the non-renewal of the contract of 
employment between the employer and the workman concerned on its expiry or of such contract 
being terminated under a stipulation in that behalf contained therein; or 


(c) termination of the service of a workman on the ground of continued ill-health; 


Also from evidence on record there is no employee employer relationship between him and the IIT is 
proved. 


In the light of above findings, the reference deserves to be answered against the Workman and is 
answered accordingly. 


No order as to cost. 
DATE:- 7/0/2025 
P.K. SRIVASTAVA, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 24 दिसम्बर, 2025

AWARD 
(Passed on this 47 day of October - 2025.) 


As per letter dated 4/08/2024 by the Deputy Chief Labour Commissioner (Central) Raipur, Government of 
India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, the reference is made to this Tribunal under section-!0 of Industrial Disputes 
Act, |947 as per reference number RP-8(I-2)/2024-ES.III dt. 4/08/2024. The dispute under reference related to :- 


“Whether the action on the part of the management of M/s. Ishwar Pratap Singh Associates, Waidhan, 
Dist. Singrauli (MP) in terminating workman Shri Radhasharan Viashwnaw, Supervisor working in 
NTPC, Sipat, Dist. Bilaspur (CG) is legal and justified ? If not, to what relief the workman is entitled to ?” 


After registering a case on the basis of the reference, notices were sent to the parties and served. None of 
the parties filed any Statement of Claim or Written Statement of Defense. The workman side never appeared. 


Since the initial burden to prove the case lies with the workman, and he has failed to discharge this 
burden, the claim is held to be not proved. Accordingly, the reference is answered against the workman.

R-47, Zone-II, M.P. Nagar,
