The General Secretary, 
Dainik Vetan Bhogi Karmchari Sangathan, 


F-, Tripti Vihar, Opp. Engg. College, 


Ujjain (M.P.) 
Workman 
Vs 

The Asstt. General Manager, 
State Bank of India, Regional Office, 
Hoshangabad Road, 
Bhopal (M.P.) 

Management 

(JUDGMENT) 


(Passed on this 067 day of November, 2025) 


As per letter dated 5/06/202 by the Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, the reference 
has been made to this Tribunal under Section-l0 of Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (in short the ‘Act’) as per 
Notification No. 7-72077/20/2077077-7)) dt. 5/06/20i2. The dispute under reference relates to:- 


“Whether the demand of the Union, Dainik Vetan Bhogi Karamchari Sangathan, for appointment of Shri 
Santosh Kumar Sahu on regular basis w.e.f. 07/70/2007 with pay is legal and justified? To what relief the 
workman is entitled?” 


After registering the case on the basis of reference, notices were issued to the parties. They appeared and 
filed their respective statement of claim in defense. 


The case of the workman is mainly that he was first appointed as a daily wage Peon on 08.03.004 by the 
Branch Manager and worked 8 hours daily till 06.0.2007 without any notice or compensation, when he put up his 
claim for regularization of his service. According to the workman union, this action of management is in violation of 
Section 25-F and 25-G of the Act, hence unjust, illegal and arbitrary. 


It is also alleged that on the basis of his continuous service with the bank for three years, he is entitled to be 
regularized and denial of his this claim by the management bank is unjust, illegal and arbitrary. He has prayed that 
holding the action of management bank in terminating his service and no regularizing his service against law, he be 
held entitled to be reinstated and entitled to be regularized with all back wages and benefits. 


The case of the management bank is mainly that the workman was never appointed against any permanent 
vacancy of peon following any recruitment process. Hence, there was no occasion of his disengagement. According to 
the Bank, he was a daily wager, engaged by Bank as and when required on intermittent basis for which he was paid 
his daily wages. He never completed 240 days in any year; management has requested that the reference be answered 
against the workman. 


॥78 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JANUARY 3, 2026/PAUSHA 3, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


In evidence, the workman has filed and proved order of Hon’ble High Court of M.P. dated 28.0.2004 in 
WP No. 48/2000, in which he was one of the petitioner’s, photocopy reply of Bank filed by them before the 
Regional Labour Commissioner during conciliation proceedings, photocopy letter of Bank dated 03.05.20i0 
regarding payment of bonus to the workman, photocopy cheque of bonus amount, which are marked as Ex-W/I to 
Ex-W/4. Workman union has further filed affidavit of the workman as his Examination-in-chief, he has been cross- 
examined by management. 


Management has filed and proved Ex-M/! and M/2 which are vouchers with respect to payment to the 
workman, management has further filed affidavit of its witnesses as his Examination-in-chief, he has been cross- 
examined by union. 


I have heard argument of Learned Counsel for workman Mr. Arun Patel and Mr. Praveen Yadav, Learned 
Counsel for the management bank. I have gone through the record as well. 


On perusal of record in light of rival arguments, following Issues came up for determination:- 


हे Whether the workman has successfully proved his continuous engagement with the management 
Bank as defined under section 25-B of the Act? 

2) Whether the action of management in disengaging the workman is legal? 

3) Subject to findings on issue No. | and 2, whether the workman is entitled to any relief? 

Issue No., 


Section 25-B of the Act, which defines ‘continuous service’, is being reproduced as follows:- 


“25B. Definition of continuous service.—For the purposes of this Chapter,— 


(7) a workman shall be said to be in continuous service for a period if he is, for that period, 
in uninterrupted service, including service which may be interrupted on account of 
sickness or authorised leave or an accident or a strike which is not illegal, or a lock-out 
or a cessation of work which is not due to any fault on the part of the workman; 


(2) where a workman is not in continuous service within the meaning of clause (7) for a 
period of one year or six months, he shall be deemed to be in continuous service under 
an employer— 


(a) for a period of one year, if the workman, during a period of twelve calendar 
months preceding the date with reference to which calculation is to be made, 
has actually worked under the employer for not less than— 


(i) one hundred and ninety days in the case of a workman employed below 
ground in a mine; and 


(ii) two hundred and forty days, in any other case; 


(b) for a period of six months, if the workman, during a period of six calendar 
months preceding the date with reference to which calculation is to be made, 
has actually worked under the employer for not less than— 


(i) ninety-five days, in the case of a workman employed below ground in a 
mine; and 
(ii) one hundred and twenty days, in any other case.” 


Pleadings of the parties on this issue have been detailed earlier. According to the workman he was appointed 
on the vacant post of Messenger in the branch in the year 99 by the then Branch Manager, he worked continuously 
for atleast 8 hours daily in the Bank. His services were earlier terminated by the Bank, he filed a writ petition No. 
48] /2000, he was reemployed by the Bank on 08.03.2004 in compliance of the order of Hon’ble High Court dated 
28.0.2004 and he worked continuously after his repayment till date of disengagement i.e., 06.l0.2007. 


He has further stated that he was paid bonus, also in his cross-examination, he has stated the he came to 
know about the vacancy from an advertisement released by the Bank, he had filled the form for appointment, he was 
interviewed by the Branch Manager, his name was not sponsored by employment ex-change, no interview letter was 
sent by the Bank. The Bank did not issue any appointment letter. 


He further states in his cross-examination that he did job of carrying documents from one counter to other, 
sent daak, carry cheques to clearing house and other works for which he was paid by the Bank. He admits that there 
was a canteen in the branch and he used to bring tea and snacks for the bank employees from the canteen. 


[भाग Ive 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जनवरी 3, 2026/07 3, 947 779 


There is on record, certified statement regarding payment to the workman which shows that during the 
period he was paid different amounts ranging from Rs. 30/- to Rs. 50/- by way of different vouchers for carrying 
cheques or other bank documents as well instruments to different places and visiting different places for bank works. 
There are also payments vouchers with respect to payment to him for filling water in the bank coolers etc. These 
vouchers show that he was paid the traveling expenses through these vouchers. When it is established that he had 
been reimbursed his travelling expenses during the period from 2004 to 2007 for visiting different places for banking 
work naturally, he would have been paid his wages also from the Bank, after all it cannot be assumed that he was 
doing this job only for reimbursement of his travelling expenses. These facts establish his engagement by the bank 
and also establish that he was engaged by the Bank in whatever capacity may be a daily wager. 


He has been paid bonus also, this fact also corroborates his statement with regard to his engagement with the 
Bank. On the other hand, there is on record affidavit of the management witness as his Examination-in-chief and his 
cross-examination in which he has stated that the workman was only assigned the work of Hamali as and when 
required basis and was paid remuneration for the work. Also that he worked only for 2 to 3 hours in a day. Infact, he 
was engaged as canteen boy by the local implementation committee for providing tea, water and snacks to the 
members, no appointment letter was issued by the Bank nor did he worked 240 days or more in any year. 


The witness also states that he did not follow the recruitment process. In his cross-examination, this witness 
states that it will be correct to say on the basis of salary payment reduced and available records that the workman was 
working in the same branch since 99 but he was working in which canteen, no record is available in this respect. He 
pleads ignorance that the Bank had taken an interview test of the workman for sub-staff in the year 997 and states 
that there is no record of employment of the workman management by local implementation committee, this witness 
further pleads ignorance regarding vacancy of peon in the said branch. 


On a comparative statement and evidence from both sides as mentioned above, it comes out that the case of 
management Bank that the workman was employed by the committee cannot be believed because there is no evidence 
except the statement of the management witness. There is no document in this respect, there is no evidence also in this 
respect. The management witness also admits that the workman used to do the Bank related works for which he was 
paid, his only point is that he did not work continuously for 240 days in a year. The documents in form of vouchers 
which show reimbursement of the workman on different dates with respect to the amount he incurred in travelling for 
the Bank work, that he worked regularly during the period from 2004 to 2007 these are the Bank certified documents. 


Thus, he is held to be a workman continuously for 240 days and more in every year including the year 
preceding the date of his disengagement as defined under section 25-B of the Act and the Bank is held to be his 
employer under the Act. 


Issue No. 4 is answered accordingly. 


Issue No. 2, 
Section 25-F & 25-G of the Act, is being reproduced as follows: 


25F. Conditions precedent to retrenchment of workmen. 


No workman employed in any industry who has been in continuous service for not less than one 
year under an employer shall be retrenched by that employer until- 


(a) the workman has been given one month's notice in writing indicating the reasons for 
retrenchment and the period of notice has expired, or the workman has been paid in lieu 
of such notice, wages for the period of the notice; 


(b) the workman has been paid, at the time of retrenchment, compensation which shall be 
equivalent to fifteen days' average pay for every completed year of continuous service or 
any part thereof in excess of six months; and 


(c) notice in the prescribed manner is served on the appropriate Government or such 
authority as may be specified by the appropriate Government by notification in the 
Official Gazette. 


25G Procedure _for_retrenchment.—Where any workman in an industrial establishment, who is a 
citizen of India, is to be retrenched and he belongs to a particular category of workmen in that 
establishment, in the absence of any agreement between the employer and the workman in this 
behalf, the employer shall ordinarily retrench the workman who was the last person to be 
employed in that category, unless for reasons to be recorded the employer retrenches any other 
workman. 


It is undisputed that no notice was issued to the workman before his disengagement nor was he paid any 
compensation. Hence his disengagement is held in violation of the act. 


Issue No. 2 stands answered accordingly. 


7720 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JANUARY 3, 2026/PAUSHA 3, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


Issue No. 3 


In light of aforesaid findings, the question arises as to relief to which the workman may be held entitled. 
Management, learned counsel has submitted that there is nothing on record to show that there was a regular vacancy 
at the time of his appointment, he was not sponsored by employment ex-change and he did not follow the recruitment 
process. Hence, his appointment is illegal. Thus he should not be reinstated on the finding forced in this argument 
from the side of the Bank. 


Learned counsel for the workman has stated that he has been working with the Bank as a daily wager since 
99. He was once disengaged in the year 2000 and was reinstated under order of Hon’ble High Court in 2004, again 
disengaged arbitrarily in 2007, he requires to be reinstated with backwages and benefits. 


There is force in the argument of management for the appointment of the workman was not through 
recruitment process, hence, not a regular appointment, a lump sum compensation quantified Rs. lLac/- in lieu of all 
his claims will meet the ends of justice which the workman is held entitled to be paid by the Bank within 30 days 
from the date of publication of the award, failing which interest @ 6% p.a. from the date of award till payment. 


In light of above discussion and findings, the reference is answered as follows.- 
AWARD 


“Holding the action of the management in disengaging the workman Santosh Kumar Sahu from 
972/0/2007, illegal and unjustified, he is held entitled to a lump sum amount of Rs. lLac/- to be paid by the Bank 
within 30 days from the date of publication award, failing which interest @ 6% p.a. from the date of award till 
payment, in lieu of all his claims.” 


No order as to cost. 
DATE:- 06--2025 
P.K. SRIVASTAVA, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 29 दिसम्बर, 2025

AWARD 
(Dated: 070 November, 2025) 


In exercise of the powers conferred by Section () & (2) of Section 2-A of Industrial Disputes (Amendment) 
Act, 20l0 (“the Act” in short), the applicant filed an industrial dispute between the employers, in relation to the 
management of State Bank of India the applicant Vajrabodhi s/o Ramchandra Meshram for adjudication vide case 
No. CGIT/NGP/APPL./03/2020-2 with the following issues framed: - 


“Whether the action of the management/ SBI by giving punishment “be brought down to lower stage 
in the scale of pay up to one stage” is legal, fair and justified? If so, what relief the applicants are 
entitled to?” 


2: Case is called out. Shri. Dadu Sachdev learned counsel for the management is present before the court but 
none is present on behalf of the petitioner. From perusal of record, it is apparent that petitioner is not responding and 
attending the court since 23.09.202l. Although, Statement of Claim and Written Statement have been filed by the 
parties respectively but the petitioner has not filed any evidence to prove his case. The petitioner is not coming to the 
court since long back. It appears that petitioner is not interested to contest the case further more. Claim of the 
petitioner is not proved. So, it is closed. 


Hence, it is ordered. 
ORDER 
“The applicants Vajrabodhi s/o Ramchandra is not entitled to any relief”. 
Justice (Retd.) SHIV SHANKER PRASAD, Presiding Officer

AWARD 

(Dated: 047 November, 2025) 


In exercise of the powers conferred by Section () & (2) of Section 2-A of Industrial Disputes (Amendment) 
Act, 20l0 (“the Act” in short), the applicant filed an industrial dispute between the employers, in relation to the 
management of Reserve Bank of India and one other and the Union “Nagpur General Workers Union for adjudication 
with the following issues framed: - 


“Whether the action of the management of Reserve Bank of India and M/s. World Wide Securities in 
terminating the services of the workman Shri. Mahipal Karia and 7 others is fair, just and proper? If 
not, to what relief the concerned workmen are entitled to?” 


2; Case is called out. Mrs Sharanya S, Manager herself is present on behalf of the respondent/management 
today in the court. But none is present on behalf of petitioner. From perusal of record, it is apparent that petitioner is 
not responding and attending the court since 08/04/2022, although Statement of Claim and Written Statement have 
been filed by the parties respectively. The petitioner has also filed his affidavit as an evidence but petitioner has not 
come to the court to prove the contents of the Statement of Claim and the contents of the affidavit filed by him. No 
other evidence has been filed by the petitioner to prove his claim. Claim of the petitioner is not proved. So, it is 
closed. 


Hence, it is ordered. 
ORDER 


“The action of the management of Reserve Bank of India and M/s. World Wide Securities in 
terminating the services of the workman Shri. Mahipal Karia and 7 others is fair, just and proper. The 
concerned workmen are not entitled to any relief” 


Justice (Retd.) SHIV SHANKER PRASAD, Presiding Officer
